*Law School of Harvard University / 2023-2024*

# Civil Procedure 2
## Professor Jim Greiner
### Fall 2023

**Available for download:** Friday, December 15 2023 starting at 8:15am EST

**Must be electronically submitted by:** 8 hours from download or by 4:30pm EST, whichever is earlier

1. The exam mode is TAKEHOME.
2. This exam is 10 pages long. Please check to see that you have all 10 pages.
3. While taking this examination you may consult any materials you desire to take this exam, but I strongly recommend that you limit yourself to the materials prepared for this 2022 Civil Procedure course. You may not discuss the examination with anyone, either while taking it or thereafter, until grades have been returned. Because some students may not take the exam on December 15, ***PLEASE DO NOT DISCUSS THIS EXAM WITH ANYONE UNTIL GRADES HAVE BEEN RETURNED***.

> By submitting your exam answer(s), you acknowledge the above instructions, and certify that the work you are submitting is your own, that you have not received unauthorized assistance on the exam, and that you have followed applicable rules, including rules for accessing reference and other materials during the exam.

4. Exam4 will automatically print your Anonymous ID and word count on the exam.
5. If you feel that there is a mistake in the exam, such as that a question is unclear, or that it refers to something that it should not, or that there is an important typographical error, then do the following. First, in your answer, explain what you believe to be the mistake. Second, explain the implications of the mistake. Third, make an assumption that is reasonable given the fact pattern and applicable law to resolve the error. Fourth, proceed with the remainder of the exam.
6. Each question's weight is specified (out of 65 total points). Time and word-count may be issues for you during the exam. Answer questions efficiently, and feel free to take into account the number of points available for each question. Don't forget your test-taking skills.
7. Do not put your name, or otherwise identify yourself, on your response.
8. Deal with all issues fairly raised, even if you think another issue would be dispositive of a matter in the real world.
9. If you believe additional information (factual or legal) is needed to deal with an issue, say so and make explicit whatever assumptions you need to proceed, but make these

<!-- page: 2 -->

assumptions reasonable and interesting. Similarly, if at any point in this question you find that you do not know what the law is on a particular subject, identify the issue as to which there is uncertainty, then discuss the various options as to what the law could or should be, noting the advantages or disadvantages of each option.
10. Feel free to use obvious abbreviations to save time and space: "SMJ" for subject matter jurisdiction; "IPJ" for in personam jurisdiction; "DJ" for diversity jurisdiction; "FQJ" for federal question jurisdiction; "CNOF" for common nucleus of operative facts; and "DPC" for due process clause.
11. All of the scenarios described below might be, and probably are, fictional; that includes the law described in this exam. Use the scenarios only for the purposes of this exam.
12. Citing authority is optional. You can get full credit without citation if you explain and apply the relevant principle. That said, citing authority could well save you time & words. For example, you might say, "Minimum contacts, *International Shoe*," instead of, "Under the test the Court articulated in *International Shoe* and subsequent cases, a court may exercise in personam jurisdiction over an objecting, non-resident defendant only if a long-arm statute authorizes such exercise, and only if the defendant has 'minimal contacts' with the forum, and only if the exercise of such jurisdiction would be fair and reasonable under the circumstances."
13. The word limit for this exam is 2500 words. If you exceed this word limit, only the first 2500 words will be graded. Good luck!

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

---

## The Law

All states relevant to this exam had a common law cause of action for negligence. To prevail on a common law negligence cause of action the Plaintiff must show: (1) The Defendant had a duty of care as to the Plaintiff, (2) that duty was breached, (3) that breach caused injuries to the Plaintiff's person or property, and (4) damage resulted to the Plaintiff. The Plaintiff must prevail on all elements to win. With respect to duty, every person owed every other person a duty to exercise ordinary care in any interaction.

All states relevant to this exam had long-arm statutes going to constitutional limits.

California law provided an affirmative defense to a negligence cause of action called "contributory negligence." As discussed in class, under this affirmative defense, if a defendant in a negligence case proved that the plaintiff's conduct was in any way negligent, and that the plaintiff's negligence caused in any part (no matter how small the contribution) the plaintiff's injury, the plaintiff received no damages. Thus, if a jury found that a defendant 99% percent at fault and a plaintiff 1% at fault for an accident in which the plaintiff suffered a $1000 injury, the plaintiff received no damages.

<!-- page: 3 -->

New York and Texas law provided a different affirmative defense to a negligence cause of action called "comparative negligence." As discussed in class, under this affirmative defense, if a defendant in a negligence case proved that the plaintiff's conduct was in some way negligent, and that the plaintiff's negligence caused in part the plaintiff's injury, the trier of fact apportioned fault between the plaintiff and the defendant, and the court awarded damages to the plaintiff in accordance with the parties' respective fault. If a jury found that a defendant was 99% percent at fault and a plaintiff 1% at fault for an accident in which the plaintiff suffered a $1000 injury, then the court would order the defendant to pay the plaintiff $990, not $0 nor the full $1000.

California and Texas used the First Restatement for horizontal choice of law.

California did not require any form of mutuality for the application of issue preclusion. Texas required that for issue preclusion to run, the parties in the second lawsuit must have been the same, and have been in the same procedural posture, as in the first lawsuit.

Remember what you already know about New York law.

In the 2015 case of *Nynaeve v. Mat*, the New York Court of Appeals (New York's highest court) held that in considering a motion for a preliminary injunction under New York state law, "a court should consider only the public interest. In our court system, we recognize that judges cannot predict the future and thus are unlikely to know at an early stage of litigation who is likely to prevail on the merits. Similarly, the harms litigants may suffer are of less importance than the interests of the public. Therefore, courts in our state in determining whether to grant a preliminary injunction motion will consider only the public interest and no other factors." In 2017, in *Egwene v. Rand*, the Supreme Court of California (California's highest court) stated, "In our state, we find the federal doctrine regarding preliminary injunctions persuasive, and we use it our state." Preliminary injunction law is procedural for horizontal choice of law purposes.

New York had four federal district courts. The Southern District of New York had jurisdiction over Manhattan and Queens in New York City. The Eastern District of New York had jurisdiction over Brooklyn in New York City. The Northern District of New York had jurisdiction over Albany, New York state's capital city. The Western District is not relevant to this exam.

Texas had four federal district courts. Austin was in the Western District of Texas. Fort Worth was in the Northern District of Texas. The other districts have no relevance to this exam.

California had four federal district courts. Sacramento was in the Eastern District of California. The other districts have no relevance to this exam.

Nevada had only one federal district court, the District of Nevada.

---

## The Characters

**Ishamael:** Ishamael operated a business out of his house in Lake Tahoe, Nevada. He never incorporated his business, preferring to own and operate everything personally. Ishamael's business involved constructing and training his buyers to deploy movable (with a sufficiently sized truck), some-assembly-required pop-up bumper boat facilities, called BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers. These constructs

<!-- page: 4 -->

consisted of small pools holding a few inches or feet of water, as well as a number of propeller-driving boats that could reach surprisingly high speeds. Ishamael had been in this business for about twenty years until the time of the events described below. In a nutshell, potential buyers of one of Ishamael's BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers paid a flat fee of $300,000 for the full set of equipment and constructs as well as a three-week course in Ishamael's large back yard on how to set up and operate it all. Once customers underwent training in Lake Tahoe, they took possession of their equipment there (Ishamael often helped to load the equipment onto customers' trucks) and were free to go wherever they liked to start their own businesses. Ishamael marketed his product exclusively on social media sites, such as Facebook, Snapchat, X, and Instagram. His Internet content was viewable anywhere in the world, and he responded to questions about his BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers from anywhere using social media.

**Tuon:** Tuon grew up in Albany, NY. After graduating from high school, she spent five years amassing a small fortune playing online poker in her parents' home in Albany before looking for a different opportunity.

**Moiraine Damodred:** Damodred grew up in Austin, Texas. After graduating from high school there, she wandered from place to place for five years, never staying in one place for longer than three months, until at age 23, she began to think about going to college. At that time, it was the fall of 2020, and she was renting a studio apartment in Brooklyn, NY, a place she decided she liked. For work, she was bartending in Manhattan. She looked for schools nearby because she especially liked the 100-square-foot apartment she was renting at the time. It took her a while to identify the schools in which she was interested, but in the early fall of 2023, she applied to Columbia University (in Manhattan), NYU (in Manhattan), Hunter College (in Manhattan), and Barnard College (in Manhattan). She also inquired with her landlord about whether they were interested in selling her the studio apartment in which she was living (Damodred figured she had saved enough for the down payment and could earn enough for the monthly payments with a full-time job). The Manhattan bar where she was bartending at the time was due to shut down for repairs during the month of November of 2023, so in the middle of October of 2023, using a computer located at the Manhattan bar, she placed notices on job board websites with national scope (meaning they allowed job seekers and potential employers from across the world to post information) seeking temporary work for that month.

**Perrin Aybara:** Perrin was born and raised in Fort Worth, Texas. As an adult, he operated a special farm raising wolves just outside Austin for use in TV shows and movies. In early 2022, on an online dating site, he met a woman named Zarine Bashere, a native of Sacramento, California, who owned a grocery store near where she grew up. They started dating. Perrin flew into Sacramento one week every month, and similarly, Zarine flew into Fort Worth one week every month.

---

## The Events

In September of 2023, Tuon came across Ishamael's business online and decided to give it a try. She borrowed the $300,000-plus she needed to get started from her parents. She paid Ishamael his fee and underwent his three-week training course in Lake Tahoe in October of 2023. The course did not include any information on how to adjust the water levels in the BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers construct to accommodate the number of boats that were floating in it. The course did, however, include several hours on how to set up the boats in the construct properly, including two hours solely on the placement of the propellers that powered the boats. In late October, Tuon took possession of the pieces of her BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers construct in Ishamael's back yard in Lake Tahoe, Nevada. She hired a

<!-- page: 5 -->

small flatbed truck and a driver. She loaded it all onto the truck, and the driver transported the whole thing to a vacant lot Tuon had rented in Sacramento, California. Tuon followed behind in her pride and joy, a fully restored red 1965 Mustang convertible that she had inherited two years earlier from an eccentric aunt. It had served as her primary means of transport ever since.

Upon arrival in Sacramento, Tuon constructed the BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers facility herself. She looked at some online job-seeking websites and found a notice there from Damodred saying that the latter was looking for temporary work. After a video interview, which Tuon conducted from Lake Tahoe and Damodred conducted on her cell phone from the Manhattan bar where she was working, Tuon offered Damodred a job. Damodred accepted on the spot. The two set up a second, 30-minute video-call, which Damodred took from a computer at her Manhattan bar, during which Tuon (in Nevada at the time) provided Damodred with information on how to operate the BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers apparatus. This was the only training Damodred received on how to operate the equipment, and it included no information on how to attach the propellers to the boats. Tuon then bought a plane ticket for Damodred to fly out of La Guardia (in Queens), NY to Sacramento. Damodred landed on the morning of November 1, 2023, and reported immediately for work.

In the afternoon of November 1, 2023, Perrin was in Sacramento visiting Zarine. While out walking around, they encountered Tuon's BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers construct. As it happened, Damodred was working, and Tuon was there keeping an eye on things. Perrin decided to give it a try. Damodred sold him a ticket and set up his boat, including the propeller. But when it came time to get in the mini-pool to drive the boat around, there was no one else present against whom to bump his boat (Zarine did not like anything energetic or exciting). At Tuon's instruction, Damodred set up another boat and got in herself.

Perrin thought the boat was great. He immediately pressed the accelerator to its maximum speed and began spinning the steering wheel from right to left, and left to right, as fast as he could. What happened next was a matter of dispute. According to Perrin, as he was trying to steer around Damodred, he felt something in the bottom of his boat catch, with the result that his boat turned sharply at top speed, throwing him clear; Damodred believed that Perrin's dangerous driving threw him clear. What was undisputed was that Perrin flew out of his boat across a short stretch of water and smashed into Damodred, pitching them both out of her boat and into the side of the BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers construct. It was also undisputed that Perrin smashed his wrist and fingers on his right hand badly, suffering multiple severe breaks that put him in the hospital for weeks. It was also undisputed that Damodred suffered a concussion and was unable to work for a month.

Immediately after the accident, while still in the hospital, Perrin contacted you, a litigator living in (wherever you want to have been living, you pick). You agreed to be his attorney. About an hour later, you received a letter attached to an email from Damodred. She found out from an angry telephone call she received from Perrin (informing her that he intended to sue her) that you were representing Perrin. Damodred's letter informed you that she intended to sue Perrin for the injuries she suffered in the accident. She alleged that Perrin drove the boat too fast and tried to turn too sharply, causing the accident. She also said that she should not be blamed for the accident because she received only minimal training on how to put the boats together and in the BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers apparatus in the second 30-minute video call with Tuon. If the accident was not Perrin's own fault, then the fault was with Tuon, who did not train Damodred properly.

<!-- page: 6 -->

While all this was going on, and throughout the litigation discussed below, Tuon continued to operate her BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers construct. It served approximately 20 customers per day.

You began to work out possible lawsuit scenarios in your head, as follows. You filed a complaint for negligence against Damodred in the United States District Court for the Southern District of New York, serving process promptly and properly. This was the complaint that you filed on Perrin's behalf.

---

### *Aybara v. Damodred*

**In the United States District Court for the Southern District of New York**

1. This Court has subject matter jurisdiction under 28 U.S.C. § 1332.
2. Venue is proper under 28 U.S.C. § 1391.
3. Plaintiff Perrin Aybara is a domiciliary of Texas. Defendant Moiraine Damodred is a domiciliary of New York.
4. On November 1, 2023, Defendant sold Plaintiff a ticket for a bumper boat ride in a BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers construct in Sacramento, CA.
5. Defendant did so despite knowing that she had received insufficient training on how to set up and operate the boats.
6. Plaintiff entered the ride in a boat that the Defendant set up. Defendant entered the ride in a separate boat that she also set up.
7. Plaintiff drove his boat safely and carefully at all times.
8. Despite Plaintiff's safe driving, Plaintiff's boat malfunctioned, causing an unexpectedly sharp turn.
9. The malfunction referred to in the previous paragraph caused Plaintiff to be thrown from the boat and into Defendant.
10. The resulting crash shattered multiple areas of Plaintiff's wrist and arm, requiring extensive hospitalization and inflicting a great deal of pain.
11. Defendant's conduct fell below the standard of ordinary care. Her negligent conduct caused Plaintiff's injuries.
12. Wherefore Plaintiff prays that this Court award Plaintiff $400,000.00 in compensatory damages and such other relief as this Court shall deem proper.

[Signature]

---

**Question 1 (14 points): Would Damodred have a basis to move to dismiss, or in the alternative to demand or to request a transfer? Explain.**

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Damodred did indeed move to dismiss or in the alternative to transfer. The SDNY Court denied the motion to dismiss, but without comment transferred the case to the United States District Court for the Eastern District of California.

<!-- page: 7 -->

You did some further investigation. You contacted a Sacramento engineering firm, asking them to take a look at Tuon's facility. You received their report two days later. According to them, there were two mistakes that led to the accident. First, the water level in the BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers construct was too low. Second, the propeller on Perrin's boat was not properly installed. It stuck too far down in the water. The combination of the two mistakes caused the propeller to hit the bottom of the construct, careening Perrin's boat off course.

You shared the report with Damodred, who was representing herself. She continued to argue that even if what the engineer said were true, Perrin's dangerous boat driving was also a cause of the accident. She contended that Perrin's conduct constituted contributory negligence that prevented Perrin from recovering anything.

Damodred filed an answer with the Court, denying liability and pleading the affirmative defense of contributory negligence, citing what she alleged to be Perrin's dangerous boat driving.

**Question 2 (4 points): Will the Northern District of California Court use contributory or comparative negligence in this case? Explain.**

<!-- uncertain: the question header refers to the "Northern District of California Court" but the transfer was to the Eastern District of California; this appears to be a typographical error in the original exam document -->

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Shortly after filing her answer, Damodred filed a timely motion with the Court seeking to implead Tuon for indemnity of anything she might have to pay, alleging that Tuon's lack of training caused any negligence on Damodred's part. In her motion, Damodred also attached a complaint proposing to sue Tuon for negligence, demanding $50,000 for the injuries she suffered in the accident. Tuon filed a brief opposing Damodred's impleader request. Tuon also stated in the brief that if the Court allows the impleader, she (Tuon) in her turn would seek to implead Ishamael, seeking to shift a maximum of $70,000 of whatever she Damodred had to pay onto him for failing to train her (Tuon) properly on the safe water levels of the BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers construct.

**Question 3 (10 points): What arguments could Tuon have made in her brief in opposition to Damodred's motion to join her (Tuon), and how was the Court likely to rule on them? Also, will the Court allow Damodred to sue Tuon for damages? If the court allowed Damodred to implead Tuon, would it also allow Tuon to implead Ishamael, if Ishamael objected? Explain.**

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

The Court granted Damodred's motion to implead Tuon, and allowed Damodred to file her complaint for damages against Tuon. It denied Tuon's motion to implead Ishamael. Tuon answered Damodred's complaint, denying that she was negligent and asserting the defense of contributory negligence (on the theory that Damodred's own negligence in setting up the boat propeller was at least a partial cause of the accident).

You continued to do further research, and you stumbled across an old (passed in 1925) federal statute called the "Amusement Park Customer's Protection Act." This federal law provided a cause of action to a plaintiff who was injured "due to the negligence of an owner of an amusement park ride." The Act defined the phrase "amusement park ride" broadly, and you were convinced that this phrase included a BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers construct. The Act further contained the following provision:

<!-- page: 8 -->

> A plaintiff seeking relief under this statute may request that a federal district court effectuate a prejudgment seizure of any assets owned by the defendant and within the jurisdictional boundaries of that federal district, with any property to be seized to be held by the court until case disposition to assure the availability of funds to pay any damages that the court may award to the plaintiff, provided that such seizure is permissible under the United States Constitution.

With Perrin's authorization, you filed a motion with the Court to add a complaint by Perrin against Tuon under the Amusement Park Customer's Protection Act, seeking $400,000.00 as well as an injunction against continued operation of Tuon's BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers facility in Sacramento on the theory that the facility was a danger to the public. At the same time, you attached a proposed motion seeking a prejudgment seizure of two assets, (i) Tuon's BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers construct itself, and (ii) Tuon's prized red 1965 Mustang car. Note that you served this motion upon Tuon, so Tuon had plenty of notice of the proposed seizure. Tuon filed a brief in opposition to the seizure.

**Question 4 (10 points): What arguments would Tuon assert to oppose your motion to add a complaint for damages against her, and how would the court analyze them? What arguments might Tuon assert in her brief in opposition to your seizure motion? Explain.**

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

The Court granted your motion to add a complaint against Tuon but denied your seizure motion. Seeking to maintain the pressure on Tuon, you filed a motion for a preliminary injunction seeking a court order prohibiting the continued operation of Tuon's BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers construct. You conceded that the continued operation of this construct would not cause Perrin irreparable harm, as he had no intention of getting in another bumper boat, but you argued that the public interest demanded that the facility be closed before more people got hurt. You noted that discovery had revealed that Tuon had taken no steps to increase the operational water levels of her BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers facility, nor trained her staff properly on the attachment of its propellers to its boats, despite serving approximately 20 customers per day.

**Question 5 (9 points): Will the Court grant your motion for a preliminary injunction? Explain.**

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

The Court denied your motion for a preliminary injunction.

**Question 6 (3 points): Can Perrin immediately appeal that denial? If so, what standard of review will the appellate court apply in reviewing the district court's denial (and does the answer to this question depend on the basis for the denial)? Explain.**

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Still seeking to maintain pressure, you moved the court for an order certifying a class of "all past, present, and future customers of Tuon's BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers facility," proposing Perrin as

<!-- page: 9 -->

the named plaintiff/class representative. The sole class relief you proposed was an injunction ordering Tuon to close permanently her BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers facility because it constituted a continuing danger to the members of the customer class.

**Question 7 (3 points): Will the Court find that Rule 23 authorizes class certification here? (Note: you need consider only Rule 23, no other Civil Procedure doctrine, in your response). Explain.**

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

The Court denied your class certification motion. After discovery, you settled Perrin's case against Tuon, leaving only Perrin's lawsuit against Damodred, and Damodred's action for contribution and for damages against Tuon. The case proceeded to a jury trial, with the judge instructing the jury on the defense of contributory negligence. The jury, answering special interrogatories, found that Perrin, Damodred, and Tuon were all negligent. The Court entered judgment in accordance with the jury's verdict, ordering that no party receive any form of damages.

The next day, Damodred sued Perrin in a Texas state court in Fort Worth, asserting that Perrin's negligence in the operation of his boat caused her own injuries, and seeking $60,000.00.

**Question 8 (5 points): What preclusion or preclusion-adjacent doctrines, if any, could either Perrin or Damodred invoke in this lawsuit? Explain.**

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

The following question has nothing to do with the hypothetical discussed previously. Consider the following statement.

> The doctrines we considered on class Day 15 (summary judgment) through and including Day 17 (trial mechanics) pose challenges vis-à-vis access to justice for litigants encountering the legal system without lawyers. Although most of the doctrines have policy values to support them, those values may not be strong enough to justify the cost to the accessibility of the system the doctrines impose. The legal community should propose changes to these legal doctrines to enhance accessibility, even if those changes require some sacrifices to the underlying policy values.

**Question 9 (7 points): Write two short paragraphs. In each of the two paragraphs, identify a change, other than the one identified in the example below, that the legal system could make to render more accessible doctrines, mechanics, or systems we covered on Days 15-17. Identify the values underlying the doctrine as it currently exists, and provide your own opinion on whether the change would be worthwhile, considering the costs and benefits associated with the change. As part of your discussion, identify who would have the power to make the change, and consider the costs in terms of time and energy needed to do so.**

Here is an example of a paragraph responsive to this prompt (again, please do not use the change identified below in your response).

<!-- page: 10 -->

The Advisory Committee on the FRCP could propose an amendment to Rule 50 (for eventual SCOTUS adoption and congressional veto consideration) such that a litigant need not move for JAMOL pre-verdict for insufficiency of the evidence to preserve the ability to do so post-verdict. The 50(a) requirement of a pre-verdict motion serves to put the non-moving party and the court on notice of a potential deficiency in the evidence to allow for (at the judge's discretion) correction, and it furthers a pre-FRCP practice of requiring a directed verdict motion prior to a JNOV motion so as to mask the fact that the entire practice of JAMOL is in tension with the 7th Amendment. These benefits are slight. Few self-interested litigants, having survived a process that includes summary judgment as a means of testing evidentiary sufficiency, will fail to produce at trial all evidence in their possession. Meanwhile, the possibility that a lawyerless litigant could lose the ability to move for JAMOL post-verdict because they did not understand Rule 50(a), while also slight, is too high to bear. In addition, the symbolic benefit of simplifying an obnoxiously complex rule is also worthwhile. In terms of transition costs, rule changes are among the least difficult changes to make; only the court system need take action.

## 2023-final · Instructor model answer

<!-- page: 1 -->

## Question 1

There is no federal CoA or issue and thus no AUJ. There is likely DJ. Perrin is a TX domiciliary (short CA trips do not establish residency), and the AIC is $400K. Damodred was born in TX, but regarding change of domicile, she (i) has been living in NY and (ii) intends to/hopes to settle down in NY, as evidenced by her only-NYC college plans plus that she alas likes Brooklyn plus that she is making inquiries to purchase real estate (Mas). <!-- uncertain: "Mas" appears to be a case citation abbreviation; original may read "Mas." as a shortened cite for Massachusetts or a domicile case --> If she has insufficient intention to stay, then she remains a TX domiciliary/citizen, and the court will dismiss (*Strawbridge*).

If Damodred is a NY domiciliary, there is general IPJ. If not, then under 4(k)(1)(A), SDNY will use constitutional-limits NY longarm. Damodred's three-year, stable residence in NY may trigger GIPJ under *Daimler*. Otherwise, SDNY will consider whether Damodred's use of computers, cell phones and physical spaces in NY to place her job-seeking ads, to conduct her job interview, and above all (given its link to the CoA) to receive the allegedly deficient video training is sufficient to constitute minimal contacts. If so, SDNY will find strong other contacts linking Damodred to NY (her residence for some years), making litigation there convenient for her, but will find NY an inconvenient place for Perrin, the situs of no relevant evidence (unless there were witnesses to Damodred's video and telephone conversations), and unlikely to be the state whose law is used (see below). Specific IPJ might fail on F&R.

(b)(1) venue is proper in the EDNY, where Damodred lives, not in the SDNY. (b)(2) venue depends on the tenuous minimal contacts to SDNY listed above. Venue is proper in the EDCal under (b)(2) (situs of the accident), so (b)(3) is inapplicable. Venue is likely improper.

Under *Iqbal*, we eliminate paragraph 11 as conclusory. Paragraph 4 describes an interaction imposing a duty of due care, paragraph 8 alleges sale of a ticket knowing that training was insufficient, paragraph 9 describes causation, paragraph 10 damages. The complaint is not just possible but plausible.

If IPJ is lacking or venue improper, the court must transfer, so long as some happy court exists; EDCal is obviously happy (4(k)(1)(A), long-arm to constitutional limits, loads of Damodred contacts including sale of ticket and allegedly negligent conduct there, F&R because CA law (see below) plus witnesses and evidence (the apparatus) plus not too inconvenient for Damodred because she worked there). The EDNY is also happy if Damodred is a NY domiciliary (GIPJ due to residence, (b)(1) venue). If SDNY is happy, SDNY may still transfer. SDNY will begin by presuming that it will honor the plaintiff's SDNY choice. Re private interest factors, there are no witnesses or evidence other than Damodred in NY, and SDNY is convenient for Damodred but not Perrin. Re public interest, there is little nexus to NY, juries will not be familiar with the situs, and NY law is unlikely to apply (see below). SDNY would analyze the same factors (minus the presumption) if it must transfer to decide where, and likely pick EDCal due to CA law as well as the presence of more witnesses (e.g., Zarine) and evidence (e.g., the construct).

## Question 2

Under the Happy Court rule (*Ferens*, *Klaxon*), if venue was improper or IPJ lacking, EDCal will use CA R1 HCoL rules, and use CA contrib law as the site of the injury. If venue was proper and IPJ present, EDCal will use NY HCoL/*Babcock* rules, presume California contrib law, and then note that Perrin-Damodred relationship was entirely in California, with no other Section 6 factors suggesting any other state because the Damodred-Perrin relationship took place entirely in CA, the evidence and witnesses are there, and the parties should expect nothing else.

<!-- page: 2 -->

## Question 3

Rule 14 allows Damodred to implead Tuon to shift liability Damodred might have to Perrin onto Tuon. If Damodred is a NY citizen (otherwise there is DJ for Damodred-Tuon), then we have the same CorC (same accident) for 1367(a), and 1367(b) is inapplicable because Damodred is not a plaintiff. No 1367(c) factors suggest declination of SSMJ. Regarding IPJ, we have 4(k)(1)(A), a CA long-arm to constitutional limits, and loads of contacts in Tuon's operating the business at issue in EDCal (providing (b)(2) venue if required), with loads of evidence (apparatus, eyewitnesses) plus a CA interest in regulating activities in its borders plus CA law for F&R. Rule 18 authorizes the negligence suit, with the same SMJ, IPJ, and venue analyses.

Rule 14 allows Tuon to implead Ishamael to shift liability to him, but AIC deficiency prevents DJ, so we look to SSMJ. Regarding same CorC, the facts are somewhat distinct in topic and time (sale and training versus construction and use) as well as space (Tahoe versus Sacramento), but they are connected by the failure to train on water levels as a cause of the accident. If the same CorC for 1367(a), then there is no 1367(b) problem because Tuon is not a plaintiff. No 1367(c) factors suggest declination of SSMJ. Venue, if necessary, is proper under (b)(2) (site of the accident). IPJ is lacking. Under 4(k)(1)(A) and the CA long-arm, Ishamael lacks minimal contacts to CA; he sold a mobile apparatus in NV, and the buyer took it somewhere out of state (WWV). <!-- uncertain: "WWV" may be an abbreviation for "World-Wide Volkswagen" (the personal jurisdiction case) used as shorthand; original likely reads "WWV" or "WWVWagen" --> His advertising is viewable everywhere, and does not target CA. Otherwise, he's never been to CA, nor transacted business there. If there were minimal contacts, then the use of CA tort law (see above), lots of evidence (apparatus, eyewitnesses) in CA, and Tuon's convenience (business setup) might render IPJ F&R, although the activity occurred out of state, Ishamael has few contacts to CA, and CA has less interest in facts that occurred in NV.

## Question 4

Rule 14 (or 20, common issue is who is at fault for the same accident t/o) authorize Perrin to sue Tuon, and a federal CoA triggers 1331 SMJ. Venue is proper under (b)(2) (accident site), and IPJ over Tuon is permissible, see above.

Although this seizure is not strictly QiR (because it is not a precursor to establishing jurisdiction), it raises all *Shaffer* concerns because the court is nevertheless seizing a piece of property and must have power to do so. In addition, the EDCal court lacks GIPJ over Tuon, and thus power what she owes cannot follow from general power over her. The power analysis is thus the same as QiR. If *Shaffer* preserved traditional quasi-in-rem (meaning *Shaffer* turned on DE's overreaching sequestration statute), then the presence of both the BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers apparatus and the Mustang in EDCal is sufficient to provide power for seizure of both. If *Shaffer* imposed *Shoe* doctrine for QiR, then the Act authorizes the seizure (analogous to a long-arm), but the Mustang bears no relationship to the CoA, and no link exists between Tuon's ownership interest in it and the CoA. The BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers apparatus is highly related to the CoA, but there is still little link to Tuon's ownership interest. The Mustang seizure is unconstitutional, and the apparatus seizure may be.

PDP doctrines, however, disallow the seizure. The alternative is not to seize and wait for an IPJ lawsuit to conclude, then follow standard effectuation of judgments procedures. Under *Mathews*, Perrin's interest only in securing payment is weak (*Doehr*) and provides no reason for urgency (Tuon is unlikely to hide or waste her livelihood or primary means of transport) with no preexisting property right; the risk of an erroneous seizure is high (a testimony-needed tort dispute (*Doehr*), with no sworn affidavit required, so

<!-- page: 3 -->

even the judge decision-maker is flying blind, and no requirement of a prompt post-seizure hearing), and Tuon's interest is high because livelihood/business and means of transport are at stake. No bond is required to reduce erroneous determination risk or protect Tuon, and no counterbond procedure is available to allow Tuon to regain possession.

## Question 5

If state law applies, and if SDNY was unhappy, EDCal will use the CA PI standard, and there is no preventable irreparable harm to Perrin; the accident has already occurred, so money is sufficient. There is no Erie conflict.

If SDNY was a happy court (see above), the EDCal will use NY procedural law including the NY PI standard. The BustYourYouKnowWhatBumpers is a constant public danger, so the PI may issue. There is an Erie conflict because if federal law applies, then just as with CA law, there is absolutely no irreparable harm, and because Tuon has a strong interest in continued access to the apparatus (her livelihood), rendering a PI unlikely. Under *York*, this difference is not outcome-determinative because merits litigation will continue regardless of the PI, use federal law. Under *Harlan*, easy-to-obtain PIs might make arguably dangerous business (amusement parks, surfing, big slides) reluctant to operate, but the connection is indirect, leaving the situation unclear. Under the *Hanna* majority, the federal law is FRCP 65, which does not abridge, enlarge, or modify a substantive right (§ 2072) because it really regulates procedure (*Sibbach*). It is applicable under the Scalia interpretation because it (under its judicial interpretation) specifies the four PI prerequisites/factors. A narrowing interpretation is hard to articulate, suggesting the same result under Ginsburg.

## Question 6

Appellate jurisdiction is available, 1292(a)(1). The court will review the Erie or venue questions, questions of law, de novo. If <!-- uncertain: "If" appears to begin a sentence that likely reads "It will review any factual findings..." — likely a typographical error in original for "It" --> will review any factual findings for clear error (Rule 52). It will review the decision of whether to issue the PI, under the correct standard, for abuse of discretion.

## Question 7

At 20 customers per day over multiple days, numerosity is present. Regarding commonality, almost any state's HCOL framework would result in CA law, and the dual threats of insufficient training and improper construction are the same for all customers. Regarding typicality and representativeness, Perrin was allegedly put in danger (and later hurt) by the propeller and the water levels. But water levels may vary day to day, as may propeller construction. In addition, the CA contrib defense makes each plaintiff's claim dependent on how that plaintiff operated their boat. Finally, we know of only one person actually hurt, Perrin. All this threatens commonality and typicality (*Wal-mart*). Perhaps all this may matter less because the class seeks an injunction only, invoking 23(b)(2). Certification thus depends on the court's view of the factual specificity of the circumstances of each plaintiff's claim and of the contrib defense; will the court focus only on the exposure to danger (all class members) or actual injuries?

## Question 8

Perrin can assert claim preclusion or a Rule 13(a) compulsory counterclaim theory to bar Damodred's lawsuit. We have the same TorO (the single accident), triggering Rule 13. The EDCal judgment was final and on the merits ("entered judgment in accordance with the jury's verdict"), and Damodred is the same party.

<!-- page: 4 -->

If that doesn't work (it will), then under *Semtek*, the Texas state court must look to the preclusion law of the state in which the EDCal is "sitting," either California or NY, with neither having a mutuality requirement. Perrin had his day in court. The EDCal jury's finding that Perrin was negligent was a focus of the EDCal litigation (actually) was the reason Perrin received no damage award (necessary). Damodred can estop Perrin from denying his own negligence.

Perrin can estop Damodred from denying her own negligence, which will keep her from recovering because Texas, and HCOL R1 state, will use CA contrib law (see above for CA HCOL analysis, same here). The EDCal jury finding of Damodred's negligence was necessary to the no-damages judgment in Damodred's action against Tuon. Damodred's negligence was central to the EDCal litigation (same issue and actually litigated), and there is no mutuality requirement.

## Question 9

Lots of ways to go, sample essay provided in question.
